		<item>
		<title>Build self-improving document processing using Amazon Bedrock AgentCore</title>
		<link>https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/build-self-improving-document-processing-using-amazon-bedrock-agentcore/</link>
		
		<dc:creator><![CDATA[Erik Cordsen]]></dc:creator>
		<pubDate>Wed, 14 Oct 2026 16:24:07 +0000</pubDate>
				<category><![CDATA[Amazon Bedrock AgentCore]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Expert (400)]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Technical How-to]]></category>
		<guid isPermaLink="false">e171b39a3994cf60cede0f83b63b3eeb1990c5b0</guid>

					<description>Learn how to build an agentic intelligent document processing (IDP) system using Amazon Bedrock AgentCore that classifies, extracts, validates, and learns from business documents with minimal human intervention, automatically handling new document formats without manual template configuration.</description>
										<content:encoded>&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href="https://warekennis.nl/wp-content/uploads/2013/11/bridging-the-information-worker-productivity-gap.pdf" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;IDC research&lt;/a&gt; shows that document-related challenges cost organizations $19,732 for each information worker annually and reduce productivity by 21.3 percent. Organizations receiving invoices from hundreds of suppliers often face layouts that vary by issuer and change without notice. Template-based systems require constant maintenance at this scale. Staff can spend up to 30 percent of their time on document-related tasks, and manual data entry error rates range from &lt;a href="https://askelie.io/manual-document-processing-costs/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;1–4 percent&lt;/a&gt;. Conventional intelligent document processing (IDP) solutions address some of this burden, but they require extensive upfront configuration for each document format and break when layouts vary across vendors.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;In this post, you learn how to build an agentic IDP system using &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/agentcore/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock AgentCore&lt;/a&gt; that classifies, extracts, validates, and learns from business documents with minimal human intervention. Amazon Bedrock AgentCore is a platform to build, connect, and optimize agents at scale, with any framework or model. Your agents automatically handle new document formats, such as supplier invoices, purchase orders, insurance claims, and loan applications. By the end of this post, you can:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Deploy a multi-agent IDP system, often in under 20 minutes.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Process new document types without manual template configuration.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Let agents train themselves on each new document format and reuse what they learn, so later documents of that type go straight to extraction without retraining.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;h2 id="challenges-with-rule-based-document-processing"&gt;Challenges with rule-based document processing&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Many IDP pipelines require you to define templates, extraction rules, and validation logic for every document format upfront. When a new vendor sends a purchase order with a different layout, the pipeline breaks. This creates three core challenges:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ol type="1"&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Template rigidity:&lt;/strong&gt; Each new document format requires manual configuration, and even minor layout changes can cause extraction failures.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;No learning:&lt;/strong&gt; The same extraction errors repeat across similar documents because the system doesn’t retain knowledge from previous corrections.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Scaling burden:&lt;/strong&gt; Each new vendor or document variant multiplies the maintenance effort, and organizations processing documents from hundreds of sources face ongoing operational costs.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ol&gt;
&lt;p&gt;An agentic approach addresses these challenges. Instead of static templates, AI agents analyze each document, generate extraction instructions, validate results, and learn from corrections automatically. Over time, your system builds a knowledge base of document formats, so you process similar documents with minimal additional configuration.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h2 id="solution-overview"&gt;Solution overview&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;In this solution, eight specialized agents collaborate across two graph-based workflows running on Amazon Bedrock AgentCore.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The following diagram shows the multi-agent workflow graph built using &lt;a href="https://strandsagents.com" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Strands Agents&lt;/a&gt;, an open source SDK for building AI agents.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div id="attachment_141321" style="width: 1005px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-141321" loading="lazy" class="size-full wp-image-141321" src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/14/Architecture-2.png" alt="" width="995" height="563"&gt;
 &lt;p id="caption-attachment-141321" class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 1: Multi-agent orchestration workflow graph&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Identification workflow&lt;/strong&gt;: The Analyzer agent extracts text using &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/textract/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Textract&lt;/a&gt; and identifies the document type and sender. The Matcher agent searches for similar documents using vector similarity in &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/s3/features/vectors/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon S3 Vectors&lt;/a&gt;, a capability of Amazon Simple Storage Service (Amazon S3).&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Training workflow&lt;/strong&gt; (new document types): When no match is found, the Instructions agent generates extraction prompts, the Extractor agent extracts structured data, and the Validator agent checks results against schemas and business rules in &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/rds/aurora/dsql/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Aurora DSQL&lt;/a&gt;. If validation fails, the Instructions Fixer agent refines the prompts and retries. On success, the Save Instructions agent persists the instructions for reuse.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Automation workflow&lt;/strong&gt; (known document types): When a match is found, the Extractor agent uses existing instructions. If validation fails, the Troubleshooter agent fixes the extracted data directly without modifying instructions.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;h3 id="how-it-works"&gt;How it works&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The following diagram shows the infrastructure architecture for the solution.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div id="attachment_141320" style="width: 999px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-141320" loading="lazy" class="size-full wp-image-141320" src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/14/Architecture-1.png" alt="" width="989" height="551"&gt;
 &lt;p id="caption-attachment-141320" class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 2: Agentic IDP infrastructure architecture on AWS&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;ol type="1"&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Business documents arrive in &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/ses/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Simple Email Service&lt;/a&gt; (Amazon SES) or direct upload to Amazon S3.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/eventbridge/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon EventBridge&lt;/a&gt; triggers an &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/lambda/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Lambda&lt;/a&gt; function that creates a tracking job in &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/dynamodb/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon DynamoDB&lt;/a&gt; and invokes AgentCore runtime, a capability of Amazon Bedrock AgentCore.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;AgentCore runtime orchestrates document processing through Strands Agents.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Your agents securely access AWS resources through machine-to-machine credentials managed by AgentCore Identity, a capability of Amazon Bedrock AgentCore, and &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/cognito/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Cognito&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Your Lambda functions become agent-compatible tools through the Model Context Protocol (MCP) interface of AgentCore Gateway, a capability of Amazon Bedrock AgentCore.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Agents extract text using Amazon Textract, classify documents using vector embeddings in S3 Vectors, and validate data against business rules in Aurora DSQL.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Agents access enterprise data through MCP servers to validate extracted content against business rules.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;You monitor status and troubleshoot issues through a chat-enabled dashboard hosted on &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/ecs/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Elastic Container Service&lt;/a&gt; (Amazon ECS).&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;You gain visibility into agent performance through AgentCore Observability, a capability of Amazon Bedrock AgentCore, and &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/cloudwatch/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon CloudWatch&lt;/a&gt; telemetry.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ol&gt;
&lt;h2 id="deploy-the-solution"&gt;Deploy the solution&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Follow these steps to deploy the solution infrastructure and web UI to your AWS account.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="prerequisites"&gt;Prerequisites&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;To deploy this solution, you must have:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;An &lt;a href="https://portal.aws.amazon.com/gp/aws/developer/registration/index.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS account&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Access to &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock&lt;/a&gt; with Amazon Nova and Anthropic (Claude) models enabled. For model availability by AWS Region, refer to &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/models-regions.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Supported models by AWS Region in Amazon Bedrock&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Access to Amazon Bedrock AgentCore (Runtime, Gateway, Identity), S3 Vectors, and Amazon Aurora DSQL in a &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/bedrock-regions.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;supported Region&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/cdk/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Cloud Development Kit&lt;/a&gt; (AWS CDK) v2 installed.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href="https://docs.docker.com/get-docker/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Docker&lt;/a&gt; installed and running (required for building and deploying container images).&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Python 3.11+ and Node.js 18+.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/iam/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Identity and Access Management&lt;/a&gt; (IAM) permissions to create S3 buckets, Lambda functions, DynamoDB tables, Cognito user pools, Amazon Elastic Container Registry (Amazon ECR) repositories, and Amazon ECS services.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;ol type="1"&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Clone the &lt;a href="https://github.com/aws-samples/aws-ai-intelligent-document-processing/tree/main/guidance/agentic-orchestration" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;repository&lt;/a&gt; and set up the environment:
  &lt;div class="hide-language"&gt;
   &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;git clone https://github.com/aws-samples/agenticidp-guidance.git
cd agenticidp-guidance

uv venv
source .venv/bin/activate
uv pip install -r requirements.txt

cd ui/orchestrator
npm install
cd ../..&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;
  &lt;/div&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Deploy the stacks:
  &lt;div class="hide-language"&gt;
   &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;uv run deploy.py --admin-email your-email@example.com&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;
  &lt;/div&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ol&gt;
&lt;p&gt;This deploys six &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/cloudformation/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS CloudFormation&lt;/a&gt; stacks: Core (S3, Cognito, DynamoDB), Gateway (Lambda tools, AgentCore Gateway), Aurora (Amazon Aurora DSQL), Agent (AgentCore runtime, containers), UI Orchestrator (API endpoints), and Modern UI (React app on &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/cloudfront/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon CloudFront&lt;/a&gt;). Deployment takes approximately 15–20 minutes.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ol start="3" type="1"&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Verify deployment by checking that each stack shows &lt;code&gt;CREATE_COMPLETE&lt;/code&gt; in the CloudFormation console.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ol&gt;
&lt;h2 id="process-files-and-review-results"&gt;Process files and review results&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;After deployment, you receive an email with the CloudFront URL, username, and temporary password.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="process-a-document"&gt;Process a document&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;ol type="1"&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Sign in to the web UI and choose &lt;strong&gt;Process Document&lt;/strong&gt;.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Upload a sample purchase order from &lt;code&gt;infrastructure/sample_data/purchase_orders/training/&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;The document enters the agent workflow:
  &lt;ul&gt;
   &lt;li&gt;The Analyzer agent extracts text and identifies the sender and document type.&lt;/li&gt;
   &lt;li&gt;The Matcher agent searches for similar documents in the vector store.&lt;/li&gt;
   &lt;li&gt;For a new document type, the training workflow generates extraction instructions, extracts data, validates it, and saves the instructions for future use.&lt;/li&gt;
   &lt;li&gt;For a known document type, the automation workflow extracts data using existing instructions and validates it.&lt;/li&gt;
  &lt;/ul&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Review results in the &lt;strong&gt;Review Jobs and Instructions&lt;/strong&gt; tab to verify extracted data, validation results, and generated instructions.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ol&gt;
&lt;h3 id="example-a-new-document-type-training-workflow"&gt;Example: A new document type (training workflow)&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Suppose you upload the first purchase order from a new retailer, Two Wheel Store. The Matcher agent searches the vector store, finds no similar document, and routes the job through the training workflow.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The Analyzer agent uses Amazon Textract to extract the raw document text:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt;
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-text"&gt;# PURCHASE ORDER
FROM: Two Wheel Store
Order #: PO-2026-0102-2584
Date: 2026-01-02

## Items Ordered
AC-021  Advanced Computer     Qty: 1   $75.61
        Colors: cr4321        @ $75.61
...
SB-004  Folding Classic       Qty: 8   $9634.32
        Colors: cb8901 I Sizes: S     @ $1204.29

TOTAL: $14931.54&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;
&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The Instructions agent generates extraction prompts from the schema, and the Extractor agent produces structured JSON:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt;
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-json"&gt;{
  "po_number": "PO-2026-0102-2584",
  "retailer_name": "Two Wheel Store",
  "order_date": "2026-01-02T00:00:00Z",
  "line_items": [
    { "sku": "AC-021", "product_name": "Advanced Computer", "quantity": 1, "unit_price": 75.61, "line_total": 75.61 },
    { "sku": "SB-004", "product_name": "Folding Classic",   "quantity": 8, "unit_price": 1204.29, "line_total": 9634.32 }
  ],
  "order_total": 14931.54
}&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;
&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The Validator agent checks the result against the schema and &lt;strong&gt;fails&lt;/strong&gt;:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt;
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-text"&gt;validation_status: INVALID
error: 'SB-004' does not match '^AB-[A-Z]{2}-\d{3}$'&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;
&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The vendor’s SKU format (&lt;code&gt;SB-004&lt;/code&gt;) doesn’t match Acme Bikes’ internal format (&lt;code&gt;AB-SB-004&lt;/code&gt;). The Instructions Fixer agent diagnoses the mismatch and adds an explicit SKU transformation rule to the extraction prompt: prefix vendor SKUs with &lt;code&gt;AB-&lt;/code&gt;. On the second pass, the seven line items validate successfully, and the Save Instructions agent persists the refined prompt to the vector store. End-to-end time for this first run: 3 minutes 49 seconds.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="example-a-known-document-type-automation-workflow"&gt;Example: A known document type (automation workflow)&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;When a second purchase order from Two Wheel Store arrives, the Matcher agent finds the stored instructions from the first run with a confidence score of 3.946 (threshold: 3.7) and routes the job through the automation workflow:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;The Extractor agent applies the stored instructions directly, so the orchestrator skips prompt generation. The SKU transformation rule learned during training applies automatically (&lt;code&gt;EL-002&lt;/code&gt; → &lt;code&gt;AB-EL-002&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;HB-071&lt;/code&gt; → &lt;code&gt;AB-HB-071&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;PD-002&lt;/code&gt; → &lt;code&gt;AB-PD-002&lt;/code&gt;).&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;The three line items validate on the first pass with an order total of $5,979.19.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;End-to-end processing completes in 1 minute 30 seconds, down from 3 minutes 49 seconds on the first run.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;h3 id="use-the-interactive-chat"&gt;Use the interactive chat&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The dashboard includes a chat interface for troubleshooting:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Ask &lt;code&gt;what tools do you have&lt;/code&gt; to see available agent capabilities.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Ask &lt;code&gt;list instructions&lt;/code&gt; to view stored extraction instructions.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Ask &lt;code&gt;what is the status of job XXXX&lt;/code&gt; to check processing progress.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Ask &lt;code&gt;what went wrong with job XXXX, how can we update the instructions?&lt;/code&gt; to diagnose failures.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;h3 id="understand-how-accuracy-improves-over-time"&gt;Understand how accuracy improves over time&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The system improves over time through two feedback loops:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Training loop&lt;/strong&gt;: When the Instructions Fixer agent refines extraction prompts after validation failures, the improved instructions are saved and reused for similar documents.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Processing loop&lt;/strong&gt;: When the Troubleshooter agent fixes extraction errors, it provides detailed analysis that helps identify patterns in document format variations.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;p&gt;When changing models, use &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock-agentcore/latest/devguide/evaluations.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AgentCore Evaluations&lt;/a&gt;, a capability of Amazon Bedrock AgentCore, to confirm that extraction quality remains acceptable. The solution already uses AgentCore Observability, so it has the required instrumentation.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h2 id="estimated-cost"&gt;Estimated cost&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;You pay only for what you use, because the solution runs on serverless and on-demand resources with no idle costs. For a workload of 10 training documents and 1,000 processed documents each month in US East (N. Virginia), the estimated cost is approximately $58 per month. Foundation model (FM) inference through Amazon Bedrock represents approximately 85 percent of the total cost, at roughly $0.055 per document. Actual cost depends largely on the models assigned to each agent. Model choices are centralized in &lt;code&gt;agents/orchestratorgraph/utils/config.py&lt;/code&gt;, so you can use lower-cost models for simpler tasks and stronger models where needed. For detailed pricing, refer to the &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/pricing/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock pricing&lt;/a&gt; page.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h2 id="customize-the-solution-for-your-use-case"&gt;Customize the solution for your use case&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;You get a purchase order processing use case with a synthetic bike company dataset, a pre-built JSON schema, and a validation Lambda function that checks extracted data against product and vendor records in a mock ERP database. To process your own documents, you must replace the included schemas and validation logic with your own.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="define-your-document-schema"&gt;Define your document schema&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Create a JSON schema that defines the fields you want to extract for each document type. The sample purchase order schema in &lt;code&gt;agents/orchestratorgraph/doc_schema/purchase_order_schema.json&lt;/code&gt; provides a reference. Your schema should specify field names, data types, and whether each field is explicitly stated in the document or inferred from context.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="build-your-validation-process"&gt;Build your validation process&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The included validator checks purchase order data against synthetic vendor and product tables in Aurora DSQL. Your production implementation requires a validation process tailored to your business rules. For example:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;An invoice validator might check vendor IDs against your accounts payable system and verify line item totals match the invoice total.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;A contract validator might verify that the document contains required clauses and that dates and party names match your records.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;A claims validator might check policy numbers, coverage limits, and claimant information against your policy management system.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;p&gt;To add a new validator, create a Lambda function that accepts extracted JSON and returns a pass/fail result with detailed error messages, then register it as a tool in AgentCore Gateway. Refer to &lt;code&gt;gateway/tools/po_validator_tool/&lt;/code&gt; for the implementation pattern.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="update-agent-prompts"&gt;Update agent prompts&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The agent prompts in &lt;code&gt;agents/orchestratorgraph/prompts/&lt;/code&gt; reference purchase order terminology. Update these prompts to reflect your document types and extraction requirements. The Orchestrator agent, Validator agent, and Instructions agent each contain document-type-specific language that you should adapt.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h2 id="best-practices"&gt;Best practices&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Keep these recommendations in mind as you train and operate your agentic IDP system.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Start with representative samples&lt;/strong&gt;: Upload 5–10 documents per type during training, including edge cases with format variations, to build reliable extraction instructions.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Use business rule validation&lt;/strong&gt;: Connect validators to your reference data (vendor lists, product catalogs) in Aurora DSQL to catch extraction errors early.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Monitor agent performance&lt;/strong&gt;: Use AgentCore Observability and CloudWatch to track execution paths, identify bottlenecks, and measure extraction accuracy over time.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Add document types incrementally&lt;/strong&gt;: Create a JSON schema for each new document type and a corresponding validation Lambda function. The agents handle the rest.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Add guardrails for production&lt;/strong&gt;: Apply Amazon Bedrock Guardrails to enforce content filtering and grounding checks on agent outputs. This is especially important when you process sensitive document types such as insurance claims or loan applications.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Review the OWASP Top 10 for LLMs&lt;/strong&gt;: Consult the &lt;a href="https://genai.owasp.org/llm-top-10/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;OWASP Top 10 for LLMs and Generative AI Apps&lt;/a&gt; for security considerations when building applications that use large language models (LLMs).&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;h2 id="clean-up"&gt;Clean up&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Important:&lt;/strong&gt; Before running cleanup, export the data you want to retain. The &lt;code&gt;destroy.py&lt;/code&gt; script permanently deletes processed documents (S3), job history (DynamoDB), validation data (Aurora DSQL), and learned extraction instructions (vector store).&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;To avoid ongoing charges, remove the deployed resources:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt;
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;uv run destroy.py&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;
&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Verify that the six CloudFormation stacks are deleted on the console: AgenticIDP-Core-Dev, AgenticIDP-Gateway-Dev, AgenticIDP-Aurora-Dev, AgenticIDP-Agent-Dev, AgenticIDP-UIOrchestr-Dev, and AgenticIDP-ModernUI-Dev.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h2 id="conclusion"&gt;Conclusion&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;In this post, you learned how to build a self-improving document processing system using Amazon Bedrock AgentCore, where specialized AI agents collaborate to classify, extract, validate, and learn from business documents. This agentic approach adapts to new document formats automatically and improves accuracy over time through feedback loops, replacing manual template configuration with autonomous learning.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;As next steps:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ol type="1"&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Deploy the &lt;a href="https://github.com/aws-samples/aws-ai-intelligent-document-processing/tree/main/guidance/agentic-orchestration" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;sample solution&lt;/a&gt; and test with the included purchase order samples.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Add your own document types by creating JSON schemas and validation rules.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Explore how to integrate extracted data with downstream systems such as ERP, CRM, or content management tools.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ol&gt;
&lt;p&gt;To learn more about the services and patterns used in this post, refer to the following resources:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;For a deep dive into the agent framework used in this solution, refer to &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/strands-agents-sdk-a-technical-deep-dive-into-agent-architectures-and-observability/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Strands Agents SDK: A technical deep dive into agent architectures and observability&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;To understand how AgentCore Gateway transforms APIs into agent-compatible tools, refer to &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/introducing-amazon-bedrock-agentcore-gateway-transforming-enterprise-ai-agent-tool-development/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Introducing Amazon Bedrock AgentCore Gateway: Transforming enterprise AI agent tool development&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;For background on the Model Context Protocol and how it integrates with AWS services, refer to &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/unlocking-the-power-of-model-context-protocol-mcp-on-aws/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Unlocking the power of Model Context Protocol (MCP) on AWS&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;To learn how agents can retain context across sessions, refer to &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/amazon-bedrock-agentcore-memory-building-context-aware-agents/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock AgentCore Memory: Building context-aware agents&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;For details on Amazon Bedrock AgentCore capabilities, refer to the &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/agentcore.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock AgentCore documentation&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;p&gt;For expert guidance on building document processing solutions, &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/professional-services/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Professional Services&lt;/a&gt; and &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/partners/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Partners&lt;/a&gt; can help.&lt;/p&gt;

			</item>
		<item>
		<title>Generate presentations with Amazon Bedrock AgentCore</title>
		<link>https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/generate-presentations-with-amazon-bedrock-agentcore/</link>
		
		<dc:creator><![CDATA[Ian Lunsford]]></dc:creator>
		<pubDate>Wed, 14 Oct 2026 13:58:43 +0000</pubDate>
				<category><![CDATA[Advanced (300)]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Amazon Bedrock AgentCore]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Technical How-to]]></category>
		<guid isPermaLink="false">d7fcbb6e54548bceb8e3c5489bb676b9bb401df4</guid>

					<description>This post walks you through building an AI agent system with Amazon Bedrock AgentCore that automates PowerPoint presentation creation from enterprise documents. A supervisor agent, Retrieval Augmented Generation, and Amazon Bedrock Guardrails generate grounded presentations in under 90 seconds.</description>
										<content:encoded>&lt;p&gt;A financial services team spends significant time each week manually creating client PowerPoint presentations from quarterly reports, compliance documents, and market analyses. Each presentation requires reading hundreds of pages, extracting key data points, structuring slides, and generating supporting visuals. This post walks you through building an AI agent system using &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/agentcore/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock AgentCore&lt;/a&gt; that automates this workflow, completing in under 90 seconds in our internal testing. This approach reduces presentation creation time while grounding content in source documents. The solution deploys a supervisor agent on AgentCore runtime, a capability of Amazon Bedrock AgentCore, using the &lt;a href="https://strandsagents.com/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Strands Agents&lt;/a&gt; framework to orchestrate document generation and summarization tools. Anthropic Claude Sonnet 4.6 (available on Amazon Bedrock) handles content creation, Amazon Titan Text Embeddings v2 powers vector search, and Stability AI Stable Image Inpaint (available on Amazon Bedrock) generates slide images. This post includes a public repository with the code assets.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h2 id="solution-overview"&gt;Solution overview&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The solution deploys a single supervisor agent on Amazon Bedrock AgentCore runtime that selects between document generation and summarization tools based on user requests. A Retrieval Augmented Generation (RAG) pipeline uses Amazon OpenSearch Serverless for vector storage and semantic search across multimodal content.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The solution includes a REST API built with .NET 8 minimal APIs, so you can integrate with existing enterprise applications through standard HTTP calls. AWS Lambda offers serverless compute, including containerized functions for PowerPoint generation. The solution secures the implementation with &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/vpc/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Virtual Private Cloud (Amazon VPC)&lt;/a&gt; isolation, &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/kms/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Key Management Service (AWS KMS)&lt;/a&gt; encryption, and &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/iam/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Identity and Access Management (IAM)&lt;/a&gt; least-privilege access control.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h2 id="key-concepts"&gt;Key concepts&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;This section covers AgentCore runtime, RAG, and content safety guardrails as they apply to this solution.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="agent-orchestration-with-amazon-bedrock-agentcore"&gt;Agent orchestration with Amazon Bedrock AgentCore&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;A single AI agent generating documents from large knowledge bases must retrieve content, structure outlines, generate text, create images, and assemble files. Rather than implementing these competing responsibilities in a monolithic function, the solution uses a tool-based architecture where a supervisor agent routes requests to specialized tools.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Amazon Bedrock AgentCore runtime provides a platform to build, connect, and optimize agents at scale, with any framework or model. The agent code runs in an isolated container with session management, built-in identity, and observability. The solution uses the Strands Agents framework (an open source SDK for building AI agents) to implement the supervisor logic.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The supervisor agent exposes two tools:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;generate_pptx:&lt;/strong&gt; Creates PowerPoint presentations by invoking the PPTX Generator Lambda function, which retrieves content from the knowledge base, generates slide outlines, creates AI images, and assembles the final document.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;generate_summary:&lt;/strong&gt; Extracts and synthesizes information from the knowledge base using RAG to deliver accurate, grounded responses.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The agent determines whether a request requires document generation, content summarization, or both, then invokes the appropriate tool. You can add new capabilities by defining new tools in the agent code without modifying existing ones.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="retrieval-augmented-generation-rag-for-enterprise-knowledge"&gt;Retrieval Augmented Generation (RAG) for enterprise knowledge&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;RAG addresses a fundamental challenge in AI systems: delivering accurate, up-to-date information grounded in enterprise data rather than relying solely on the model’s training data. The implementation uses &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/knowledge-base.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock Knowledge Bases&lt;/a&gt;, which combine vector search with large language models (LLMs) to retrieve relevant context before generating responses.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The RAG pipeline works as follows:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ol type="1"&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;You upload enterprise documents to &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/s3/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Simple Storage Service (Amazon S3)&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;The system chunks and parses documents, applying special handling for multimodal content including tables, charts, and images.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Amazon Titan Text Embeddings v2 converts each chunk into high-dimensional vectors that capture semantic meaning.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/opensearch-service/latest/developerguide/serverless.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon OpenSearch Serverless&lt;/a&gt; stores the vectors, creating a searchable vector database.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;At query time, the system converts the user’s request into an embedding and performs similarity search to retrieve relevant document chunks.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Retrieved chunks supply context to the language model, which generates responses grounded in the source material.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ol&gt;
&lt;p&gt;This approach is designed to help reduce hallucinated content by grounding generated documents in source information from the enterprise knowledge base.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Note:&lt;/strong&gt; The Amazon OpenSearch Serverless collection data plane must remain publicly accessible because Amazon Bedrock Knowledge Bases accesses Amazon OpenSearch Serverless from AWS-managed infrastructure, not through customer VPC endpoints. IAM data access policies protect the collection, and VPC-only access restricts the Amazon OpenSearch Serverless dashboard.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="amazon-bedrock-guardrails-for-content-safety"&gt;Amazon Bedrock Guardrails for content safety&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Enterprise AI applications require content moderation to meet organizational standards and compliance requirements. &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/guardrails/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock Guardrails&lt;/a&gt; offers built-in content filtering and safety mechanisms integrated throughout the document generation pipeline.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Guardrails operate at multiple stages:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Input processing:&lt;/strong&gt; Filters user requests to help prevent injection issues and inappropriate content.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Output generation:&lt;/strong&gt; Monitors model outputs to verify compliance with content policies.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Personally identifiable information (PII) handling:&lt;/strong&gt; Detects and anonymizes or blocks PII.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The implementation creates custom guardrails through &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/cdk/v2/guide/cli.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Cloud Development Kit (AWS CDK)&lt;/a&gt;, configured with specific policies tailored to enterprise document creation. The agent applies guardrails at the model invocation level, providing consistent content safety without requiring manual configuration for each deployment.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h2 id="solution-architecture"&gt;Solution architecture&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The following diagram illustrates the agent-based document generation system.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/09/ML-19250-1.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/09/ML-19250-1.png" alt="Architecture diagram of the Amazon Bedrock AgentCore presentation generation system, showing request flow through Amazon API Gateway and AWS Lambda to the supervisor agent, knowledge bases, and storage within a VPC" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 1: Architecture of the Amazon Bedrock AgentCore RAG presentation generation system&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The data flow begins with user requests arriving through &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/apigateway/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon API Gateway&lt;/a&gt;, which routes them to a .NET-based Chat API running on &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/lambda/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Lambda&lt;/a&gt;. The API maintains conversation state in &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/dynamodb/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon DynamoDB&lt;/a&gt;. It then invokes the supervisor agent hosted on Amazon Bedrock AgentCore runtime.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The supervisor agent analyzes the request and selects the appropriate tool. For document generation, it invokes the PPTX Generator Lambda (a containerized Python function). For summarization, the supervisor invokes the Summary Generator Lambda, which queries the knowledge base through the &lt;code&gt;retrieve_and_generate&lt;/code&gt; API.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The system processes, chunks, and embeds documents stored in Amazon S3 using Amazon Titan Text Embeddings v2. Amazon OpenSearch Serverless indexes the embeddings for fast semantic search. When tools query the knowledge base, Amazon OpenSearch Serverless retrieves relevant chunks, which are then supplied as context to Anthropic Claude Sonnet 4.6 (on Amazon Bedrock) for generating grounded responses.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The system stores generated documents in Amazon S3 and returns presigned URLs for secure download. Security measures include:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/waf/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS WAF&lt;/a&gt; with managed rule sets and IP-based rate limiting on Amazon API Gateway.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/cognito/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Cognito&lt;/a&gt; with the Plus feature plan and advanced security mode for threat protection.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Amazon Bedrock Guardrails provisioned through AWS CDK for consistent content filtering.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Amazon DynamoDB with point-in-time recovery, deletion protection, and customer-managed AWS KMS encryption.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Amazon S3 buckets with versioning, server access logging, and block public access.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/xray/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS X-Ray&lt;/a&gt; tracing on Lambda functions for distributed debugging.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Input validation with regex-based tenant ID and session ID verification.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Tenant isolation through &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/STS/latest/APIReference/Welcome.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Security Token Service (AWS STS)&lt;/a&gt; role assumption per tenant.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;h2 id="prerequisites"&gt;Prerequisites&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Before deploying, you need the following tools and access configured in your environment:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;AWS environment:&lt;/strong&gt;
  &lt;ul&gt;
   &lt;li&gt;An active AWS account with permissions for deploying infrastructure.&lt;/li&gt;
   &lt;li&gt;AWS Command Line Interface (AWS CLI) configured with credentials.&lt;/li&gt;
   &lt;li&gt;IAM role with appropriate service access policies.&lt;/li&gt;
  &lt;/ul&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Development tools:&lt;/strong&gt;
  &lt;ul&gt;
   &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/core/whats-new/dotnet-8/overview" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;.NET SDK 8&lt;/a&gt; with Amazon.Lambda.Tools installed globally.&lt;/li&gt;
   &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href="https://docs.python.org/3/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Python 3.12 or later&lt;/a&gt; for AWS CDK and Lambda functions.&lt;/li&gt;
   &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href="https://nodejs.org/docs/latest/api/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Node.js&lt;/a&gt; and &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/cdk/v2/guide/cli.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS CDK CLI&lt;/a&gt; for infrastructure deployment.&lt;/li&gt;
   &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href="https://docs.docker.com/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Docker&lt;/a&gt; or &lt;a href="https://runfinch.com/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Finch&lt;/a&gt; for building container images (ARM64 support required).&lt;/li&gt;
  &lt;/ul&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;AWS services access:&lt;/strong&gt;
  &lt;ul&gt;
   &lt;li&gt;Amazon Bedrock with model access for Anthropic Claude Sonnet 4.6, Amazon Titan Text Embeddings v2, and Stability AI Stable Image Inpaint (available through Amazon Bedrock). For model availability by Region, see &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/models-regions.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Supported models by AWS Region in Amazon Bedrock&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/li&gt;
   &lt;li&gt;Amazon Bedrock AgentCore access enabled in your account.&lt;/li&gt;
   &lt;li&gt;Amazon API Gateway Regional REST API integration timeout configured to at least 300 seconds. The default is 29 seconds. For more information, see &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/apigateway/latest/developerguide/api-gateway-execution-service-limits-table.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;API Gateway quotas&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/li&gt;
   &lt;li&gt;An existing Amazon VPC with private subnets.&lt;/li&gt;
   &lt;li&gt;An Amazon S3 bucket for deployment artifacts.&lt;/li&gt;
  &lt;/ul&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;h2 id="set-up-the-solution"&gt;Set up the solution&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The setup process includes configuring deployment parameters and initializing the AWS CDK environment.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ol type="1"&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Clone the repository&lt;/strong&gt; and navigate to the project directory:
  &lt;div class="hide-language"&gt;
   &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;git clone https://github.com/aws-samples/sample-generating-powerpoint-presentations-with-amazon-bedrock.git&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;
  &lt;/div&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Create the configuration file&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;code&gt;pipeline-params.ini&lt;/code&gt; at the repository root:
  &lt;div class="hide-language"&gt;
   &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-ini"&gt;environment=dev
awscliprofile=
ContainerRegistry=lambda-pptx-gen
AgentContainerRegistry=agentcore-supervisor
BucketDev=&amp;lt;amzn-s3-demo-bucket&amp;gt;
VpcId=&amp;lt;your-vpc-id&amp;gt;
ParsingInferenceProfileId=us.anthropic.claude-haiku-4-5-20251001-v1:0
AgentInferenceProfileId=us.anthropic.claude-sonnet-4-6
EmbeddingModelId=amazon.titan-embed-text-v2:0
ImageGenerationModelId=stability.stable-image-inpaint-v1:0
PPTXGenImageTag=latest
AgentImageTag=latest&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;
  &lt;/div&gt;
  &lt;p&gt;Set &lt;code&gt;awscliprofile&lt;/code&gt; to your AWS CLI profile name, or leave empty to use environment variable credentials.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Bootstrap AWS CDK&lt;/strong&gt; (one-time per account and Region):
  &lt;div class="hide-language"&gt;
   &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;cdk bootstrap&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;
  &lt;/div&gt;
  &lt;p&gt;This creates the &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/cloudformation/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS CloudFormation&lt;/a&gt; CDKToolkit stack required for AWS CDK deployments.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Make build scripts executable:&lt;/strong&gt;
  &lt;div class="hide-language"&gt;
   &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;chmod +x ./pipeline-build.sh
chmod +x ./pipeline-deploy.sh
chmod +x ./deploy-agentcore.sh&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;
  &lt;/div&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ol&gt;
&lt;h2 id="build-and-deploy"&gt;Build and deploy&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The deployment consists of three phases: building artifacts, deploying infrastructure, and deploying the AgentCore runtime agent.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="build-phase"&gt;Build phase&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The build phase compiles application code and packages deployment artifacts. Run the build script:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt;
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;./pipeline-build.sh&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;
&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;This compiles the .NET projects (Chat API) and packages them as Lambda deployment packages. It builds the PPTX Generator container image using Docker or Finch and authenticates with &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/ecr/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Elastic Container Registry (Amazon ECR)&lt;/a&gt;. It then pushes the container image and stores build artifacts in the &lt;code&gt;asset-output&lt;/code&gt; directory.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="infrastructure-deployment-phase"&gt;Infrastructure deployment phase&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The infrastructure deployment creates AWS resources and configures service integrations. Run the deployment script:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt;
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;./pipeline-deploy.sh&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;
&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The deployment creates four AWS CloudFormation stacks in dependency order:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;VPCEndpointsStack:&lt;/strong&gt; Configures Amazon VPC endpoints for AWS services including Amazon Bedrock AgentCore.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;OpensearchStack:&lt;/strong&gt; Provisions Amazon OpenSearch Serverless collection for vector storage.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;AgentCoreStack:&lt;/strong&gt; Creates guardrails, Lambda functions, IAM roles, and stores configuration in &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/systems-manager/latest/userguide/systems-manager-parameter-store.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Systems Manager Parameter Store&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;ChatApiStack:&lt;/strong&gt; Deploys Amazon API Gateway and Chat API Lambda.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;h3 id="agentcore-runtime-deployment"&gt;AgentCore runtime deployment&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;After the infrastructure stacks are deployed, deploy the supervisor agent to AgentCore runtime:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt;
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;./deploy-agentcore.sh&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;
&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;This script builds the supervisor agent container (ARM64), pushes it to Amazon ECR, creates the AgentCore runtime and endpoint, and updates the Systems Manager parameter with the endpoint Amazon Resource Name (ARN). The Chat API references this endpoint ARN to route requests to the agent.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;After deployment, AWS CloudFormation outputs include the Amazon API Gateway endpoint URL and resource identifiers.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h2 id="configuration"&gt;Configuration&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The solution offers several configuration points:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="agent-instructions"&gt;Agent instructions&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Edit &lt;code&gt;agent/app.py&lt;/code&gt; to modify the supervisor agent’s system prompt and behavior. The agent uses Strands Agents with two tools defined in &lt;code&gt;agent/tools.py&lt;/code&gt;:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;generate_pptx:&lt;/strong&gt; Invokes the PPTX Generator Lambda for document creation.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;generate_summary:&lt;/strong&gt; Invokes the Summary Generator Lambda for knowledge base queries.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;h3 id="model-selection"&gt;Model selection&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;In &lt;code&gt;pipeline-params.ini&lt;/code&gt;, adjust model identifiers:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;code&gt;ParsingInferenceProfileId&lt;/code&gt;: Model for knowledge base document parsing (Anthropic Claude Haiku 4.5 recommended for cost efficiency).&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;code&gt;AgentInferenceProfileId&lt;/code&gt;: Model for agent reasoning (Anthropic Claude Sonnet 4.6 recommended).&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;code&gt;EmbeddingModelId&lt;/code&gt;: Embedding model for vector search (Amazon Titan Text Embeddings v2).&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;code&gt;ImageGenerationModelId&lt;/code&gt;: Model for slide image generation (Stability AI Stable Image Inpaint).&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;p&gt;With inference profiles instead of direct model IDs, requests draw on a broader multi-Region capacity pool, which increases throughput and helps maintain consistent performance under load.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="lambda-configuration"&gt;Lambda configuration&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Adjust compute resources in the stack files:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Chat API:&lt;/strong&gt; 128 MB memory, 29-second timeout, 100 reserved concurrency (in &lt;code&gt;api.py&lt;/code&gt;).&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;PPTX Generator:&lt;/strong&gt; 900 MB memory, 300-second timeout, 50 reserved concurrency (in &lt;code&gt;pptx_gen.py&lt;/code&gt;).&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Summary Generator:&lt;/strong&gt; 512 MB memory, 300-second timeout, 50 reserved concurrency (in &lt;code&gt;sum_gen.py&lt;/code&gt;).&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;h2 id="results"&gt;Results&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;In internal testing with a 90-page &lt;a href="https://ir.aboutamazon.com/annual-reports-proxies-and-shareholder-letters/default.aspx" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon 2024 Annual Report&lt;/a&gt; PDF, the solution produced the following performance metrics:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Presentation generation time:&lt;/strong&gt; 70–90 seconds end-to-end (including knowledge base retrieval, content generation, image creation, and file assembly).&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Slide count:&lt;/strong&gt; Six slides with title, bullet points, image+text, image-only, and takeaway formats.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Image generation:&lt;/strong&gt; Two AI-generated images per presentation using Stability AI Stable Image Inpaint.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Cost per generation:&lt;/strong&gt; Approximately $0.04 in model invocation costs at time of writing in us-east-1 (text and image generation, with image generation accounting for the majority of cost).&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Knowledge base ingestion:&lt;/strong&gt; Under 2 minutes for a single 1.3 MB PDF document.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;

&lt;p&gt;Generated presentations include:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Title slides with customizable branding.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Content slides with bullet points and structured text.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Image slides with AI-generated visuals from Stability AI Stable Image Inpaint (on Amazon Bedrock).&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Speaker notes with presentation guidance.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Consistent formatting based on corporate templates.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;h3 id="summaries"&gt;Summaries&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Summary outputs provided to the knowledge base include:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Concise synthesis of knowledge base content.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Citation tracking to source documents.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Structured formatting with key points highlighted.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;h3 id="api-responses"&gt;API responses&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The Chat API returns:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Streaming responses for real-time user feedback. Because internal testing showed that presentation generation takes 70–90 seconds (exceeding the default Amazon API Gateway 29-second integration timeout), the Chat API uses an asynchronous invocation pattern. Use streaming or polling to receive the final result rather than waiting on a synchronous REST call.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Conversation context maintenance across multiple turns.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Amazon S3 presigned URLs for document downloads (5-minute expiration).&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Generated content undergoes Amazon Bedrock Guardrails validation to verify compliance with content policies before delivery.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h2 id="clean-up"&gt;Clean up&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;To avoid ongoing charges, delete the deployed resources:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ol type="1"&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Delete the AgentCore runtime&lt;/strong&gt; and endpoint:
  &lt;div class="hide-language"&gt;
   &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;aws bedrock-agentcore-control delete-agent-runtime-endpoint \
    --agent-runtime-id &amp;lt;runtime-id&amp;gt; \
    --agent-runtime-endpoint-id &amp;lt;endpoint-id&amp;gt;

aws bedrock-agentcore-control delete-agent-runtime \
    --agent-runtime-id &amp;lt;runtime-id&amp;gt;&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;
  &lt;/div&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Turn off Amazon DynamoDB deletion protection&lt;/strong&gt; (required before stack deletion):
  &lt;div class="hide-language"&gt;
   &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;aws dynamodb update-table --table-name chat-sessions-dev --no-deletion-protection-enabled
aws dynamodb update-table --table-name tenant-table-dev --no-deletion-protection-enabled&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;
  &lt;/div&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Delete AWS CloudFormation stacks&lt;/strong&gt; in reverse dependency order:
  &lt;div class="hide-language"&gt;
   &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;aws cloudformation delete-stack --stack-name ChatApiStack
aws cloudformation delete-stack --stack-name AgentCoreStack
aws cloudformation delete-stack --stack-name OpensearchStack
aws cloudformation delete-stack --stack-name VPCEndpointsStack&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;
  &lt;/div&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Empty Amazon S3 buckets&lt;/strong&gt; before deletion (AWS CloudFormation cannot delete non-empty buckets):
  &lt;div class="hide-language"&gt;
   &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;aws s3 rm s3://amzn-s3-demo-bucket --recursive&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;
  &lt;/div&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Delete Amazon ECR repositories&lt;/strong&gt; containing container images:
  &lt;div class="hide-language"&gt;
   &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;aws ecr delete-repository --repository-name lambda-pptx-gen --force
aws ecr delete-repository --repository-name agentcore-supervisor --force&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;
  &lt;/div&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Delete tenant knowledge base sub-stacks&lt;/strong&gt; (created by the tenant provisioner):
  &lt;div class="hide-language"&gt;
   &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;aws cloudformation delete-stack --stack-name bedrock-kb-&amp;lt;tenant-id&amp;gt;&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;
  &lt;/div&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ol&gt;
&lt;h2 id="security-considerations"&gt;Security considerations&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;This solution uses Lambda environment variables for configuration values such as model IDs, Amazon S3 bucket names, and guardrail IDs. These are resource identifiers, not secrets. For production deployments, use AWS Secrets Manager for sensitive configuration values such as API keys and database credentials.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The AgentCore runtime agent runs in an isolated container with session-level isolation. Each user session operates in a dedicated environment, helping to prevent cross-session data contamination.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h2 id="conclusion"&gt;Conclusion&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;In this post, you built an AI agent system with Amazon Bedrock AgentCore that automates enterprise document generation. The supervisor agent deployed on AgentCore runtime routes requests to specialized tools for document creation and summarization, reducing presentation creation to about 90 seconds in an internal test.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The RAG pipeline grounds generated content in source documents, and Amazon Bedrock Guardrails verifies content compliance. You can adapt this architecture for automated report generation in financial services, technical documentation for engineering teams, or proposal creation for sales organizations.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;To get started, clone the &lt;a href="https://github.com/aws-samples/sample-generating-powerpoint-presentations-with-amazon-bedrock" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;GitHub repository&lt;/a&gt; and follow the setup instructions. For more information about the services used, refer to the &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock-agentcore/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock AgentCore documentation&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href="https://strandsagents.com/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Strands Agents SDK&lt;/a&gt;, and &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/cdk/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS CDK Developer Guide&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p style="clear: both"&gt;&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;hr style="width: 100%"&gt;

			</item>
		<item>
		<title>How Tamnoon built AI-powered cloud security remediation with Amazon Bedrock</title>
		<link>https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/how-tamnoon-built-ai-powered-cloud-security-remediation-with-amazon-bedrock/</link>
		
		<dc:creator><![CDATA[Ishara Premadasa]]></dc:creator>
		<pubDate>Wed, 14 Oct 2026 13:53:33 +0000</pubDate>
				<category><![CDATA[Amazon Bedrock]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Customer Solutions]]></category>
		<guid isPermaLink="false">e450e4e25d2199a8263f8d40fbee33f9f2e8a25b</guid>

					<description>Learn how Tamnoon built Tami, an agentic cloud remediation orchestrator on Amazon Bedrock that autonomously investigates and safely remediates cloud security findings from CNAPP and cloud-native sources. The approach drove a 90%+ reduction in critical cloud exposure in 90 days with zero production issues.</description>
										<content:encoded>&lt;p&gt;Critical cloud exposures pile up faster than security teams can close them. Cloud-native application protection platforms (CNAPP), cloud providers, and posture scanners generate more findings than any team can triage, and risk accumulates in the gap between detected and fixed. Most findings sit untouched, not because they’re hard to fix. Someone must investigate context, identify the owner, confirm the fix won’t break production, and then do it at a volume that outpaces headcount.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;In this post, we share how &lt;a href="https://www.tamnoon.io/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Tamnoon&lt;/a&gt; built Tami, an agentic cloud remediation orchestrator on &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock&lt;/a&gt;, to close that gap. Tami autonomously investigates findings ingested from CNAPP and cloud-native sources and assesses whether remediation is safe. It then generates and orchestrates the fix with guardrails and governance wherever a change touches a customer’s cloud. The approach &lt;a href="https://tamnoon.io/blog/how-tamnoon-rebuilt-cloud-remediation/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;drove an over 90 percent reduction&lt;/a&gt; in critical cloud exposure in 90 days with zero production issues.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h2 id="about-tamnoon"&gt;About Tamnoon&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Tamnoon is an AI-powered cloud security remediation platform that works alongside CNAPPs. Its AI assistant, Tami, helps human cloud security experts investigate, prioritize, and safely remediate misconfigurations and issues across customer environments in AWS, Azure, Google Cloud, and other cloud providers.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Tamnoon integrates with the CNAPPs and cloud-native tools teams already use and turns their findings into validated, production-safe fixes. Most of the work including investigation, prioritization, and remediation planning happens before anything touches a live environment, which is what makes automated remediation trustworthy at scale.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h2 id="cloud-security-remediation-challenges"&gt;Cloud security remediation challenges&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;A security engineer can spend hours investigating a single finding. Often, it traces back to the same root cause as dozens of other alerts.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Alert volume overwhelms headcount: CNAPPs and scanners generate thousands of findings. Teams can’t triage them all, so critical exposures sit unresolved while risk compounds for months.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Investigation is manual and repetitive: Each finding requires context gathering on who owns the resource, what depends on it, whether the fix is safe. This eats hours of senior engineer time, repeated across every item in the backlog.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Fear of production impact blocks automation: Teams hesitate to automate remediation because they can’t risk taking down production. Without confidence that a fix is safe, nothing moves.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Reactive prioritization: Without a way to cluster related findings or score remediation confidence, teams work through alerts linearly rather than addressing root causes that resolve many issues at once.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Tamnoon’s solution addresses the full pipeline from finding ingestion to validated fix. The platform treats these challenges as interconnected rather than isolated problems.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h2 id="solution-overview"&gt;Solution overview&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Tamnoon takes you from initial finding to executing the fix. The platform ingests findings from CNAPP, Cloud Security Posture Management (CSPM) providers, and other security tools and normalizes them into a unified schema. It then enriches them with asset and exposure context and prioritizes them. From there, the agentic layer takes over using a set of specialized AI agents, each running in an event-driven, serverless architecture on AWS, so every stage scales independently and fails safely.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="agentic-architecture-on-amazon-bedrock"&gt;Agentic architecture on Amazon Bedrock&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The platform delegates work to four specialized agents, each responsible for a distinct phase of the remediation pipeline:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;An investigation agent draws on training from millions of real customer cloud environments to determine context, filters that context through a large language model (LLM)-as-a-judge validation layer. It reasons through the remediation goal, operational scope, and cloud-provider-specific verification, producing read-only investigation steps and a complete investigation plan.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;A safety-scoring agent builds an evidence chain per asset, combining automated verification results with analyst findings. It classifies each finding’s remediation as SAFE, RISKY, or AWAITING DATA with a deliberately conservative bias. When in doubt, it downgrades.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;A remediation agent activates only for findings that the safety agent has deemed safe. It reasons through ordering, dependencies, and conditions, then produces a remediation plan in which every step follows a backup, remediate, and verify structure and includes rollback guidance.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;A code-generation agent turns plan steps into executable remediation, but only after an input guardrail screens for relevance and prompt injection. Every artifact passes an automated generate-and-review loop in which a separate reviewer agent must approve the output.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;h3 id="why-amazon-bedrock"&gt;Why Amazon Bedrock&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;When the Tamnoon team set out to build Tami, the choice of foundation model (FM) platform was going to shape everything that followed. Tamnoon’s remediation agents don’t all do the same kind of work. Some perform deep investigative reasoning to understand the root cause of a security finding. Others handle high-volume validation, checking proposed fixes quickly and cheaply. Still others review generated code, balancing quality against cost on every task. Matching each of these jobs to the right model, without stitching together a different integration for each one, was a core requirement.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;That requirement is what made Amazon Bedrock the natural fit. Through a single API, each agent can call the foundation model best suited to its task. The most capable models handle the hardest reasoning. More efficient models take on the repetitive, high-throughput work. Review-focused models optimize the trade-off between output quality and cost. Because it’s one integration rather than many, models can be swapped or upgraded as the landscape evolves, without re-plumbing the architecture each time.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Operational overhead at scale was another factor in the decision. As request volume grew, Tamnoon wanted to avoid building and maintaining its own logic to distribute traffic across regional capacity. Amazon Bedrock Cross-Region inference handles this automatically. It automatically routes inference requests to the optimal AWS Region within a defined geographic boundary, maintaining throughput without requiring any custom routing code to operate.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Security was the final consideration, and for a cloud security company it was non-negotiable. With Bedrock, inference runs inside Tamnoon’s own AWS environment under AWS Identity and Access Management (IAM)-scoped permissions. Sensitive customer data never leaves the trust boundary. That gave the team confidence that the platform underpinning Tami met the same standard Tamnoon holds its own service to.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="how-tamnoon-integrates-with-cnapps-and-cloud-security-tools"&gt;How Tamnoon integrates with CNAPPs and cloud security tools&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Tamnoon sits downstream of the detection tools security teams already run. CNAPPs like Wiz, CrowdStrike, Palo Alto Cortex, Upwind, SentinelOne, Orca, and Cyera along with cloud-native services like AWS Security Hub and Amazon GuardDuty are the source of truth for what’s wrong in the environment. Tamnoon connects through API-based integrations and normalizes findings into a common format, so a misconfiguration looks the same whether it came from Wiz or GuardDuty. The detection tools remain the foundation. Tamnoon delivers the end-to-end remediation that follows.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;For every finding it works, Tami assigns a Remediation Confidence Indicator (RCI) of SAFE, RISKY, or AWAITING DATA. That rating tells teams what can be automated and what needs human review first.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/12/ML-21356-1-1.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/12/ML-21356-1-1.png" alt="Tamnoon’s Tami interface assigning Remediation Confidence Indicator ratings to cloud security findings" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 1: Tamnoon in action&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;h3 id="tamnoon-aws-architecture"&gt;Tamnoon AWS architecture&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Tamnoon runs entirely within its own AWS accounts, isolated per environment. Users access the platform through a single secure entry point built on Amazon API Gateway, with encryption managed by AWS Certificate Manager and sign-in handled by Amazon Cognito. Everything behind that entry point runs in private networks across multiple Availability Zones, so the core platform is never directly exposed to the internet.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Inside that environment, Tamnoon combines two kinds of compute. Amazon Elastic Kubernetes Service (Amazon EKS) runs the always-on services behind the dashboard, while AWS Lambda powers the event-driven work of collecting findings, analyzing them, and executing remediations. A set of AWS messaging and workflow services, including Amazon EventBridge, Amazon Simple Queue Service (Amazon SQS), and AWS Step Functions, coordinate these steps so large volumes of findings are processed reliably and in parallel, with no single bad record stalling the rest.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The AI layer runs on Amazon Bedrock. &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/agentcore/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock AgentCore&lt;/a&gt;, a platform to build, connect, and optimize agents at scale with any framework or model, gives Tamnoon’s agents a governed way to take action instead of open-ended access. Amazon Bedrock Guardrails then review both the request and the generated code before anything moves forward. Supporting services handle the rest of the platform: managed databases and caching for state, Amazon Simple Storage Service (Amazon S3) for playbooks and artifacts, and AWS Secrets Manager for credentials, so sensitive keys never live in application code.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;On the customer side, everything to the left of the AWS Cloud boundary is what customers connect into Tamnoon. They can link their own AWS, Azure, and Google Cloud accounts, plug in the security tools their findings come from, and connect the destinations where they want outcomes delivered, such as Slack, Jira, ServiceNow, and GitHub.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/12/ML-21356-2-1.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/12/ML-21356-2-1.png" alt="Tamnoon AWS architecture with customer connections, an Amazon API Gateway entry point, Amazon EKS and AWS Lambda compute, messaging services, and the Amazon Bedrock AI layer" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 2: Tamnoon AWS architecture&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;h3 id="how-tamnoon-maintains-zero-production-issues"&gt;How Tamnoon maintains zero production issues&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Zero production issues is a reality for Tamnoon’s customers, and it is no accident. Autonomy at Tamnoon sits inside a defense-in-depth safety architecture, where each layer assumes the one before it might miss something.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;It starts before any fix is even drafted. No remediation is written until an independent safety assessment, focused on scope and deliberately biased toward caution, concludes that the change is safe to make. From there, the platform draws a hard line between reading and writing. Investigation steps are validated as strictly read-only, so the work of understanding a problem can never alter it. Only the remediation path is allowed to change anything, and every change it makes carries its own backup, verification, and rollback steps.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;That discipline is reinforced by constant self-critique. Each generation step is paired with a reflection step that checks the output and feeds its critique back into regeneration, so the system is always reviewing its own work rather than trusting it outright. Those recommendations are also kept grounded in reality. Agent output is validated against automation results, user feedback, and training data drawn from real fixed alerts, and when the model cannot reach an accurate result on its own, a human is brought into the loop.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The final safeguard is at the moment of execution. The AI never holds cloud credentials. Instead, execution is brokered through short-lived, tenant-scoped credentials under customer-controlled trust, running in an isolated environment kept separate from the agents that plan the work. Wrapping all of this is human governance: remediations flow through an approval workflow with a full audit trail, and every AI capability is proven internally before it ever reaches a customer.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h2 id="data-security-and-compliance"&gt;Data security and compliance&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Tami reasons over sensitive customer data: alert payloads, resource identifiers, misconfigurations, and remediation context. That data must stay inside the AWS trust boundary, run with least privilege, and stay auditable. Standardizing on Amazon Bedrock makes that possible.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="sensitive-content-stays-inside-aws"&gt;Sensitive content stays inside AWS&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Every Tami workload invokes Anthropic Claude on Amazon Bedrock through the &lt;code&gt;ChatBedrockConverse&lt;/code&gt; interface, rather than calling a third-party LLM endpoint. That single choice puts the full AI supply chain under one AWS native service, which has real consequences for how customer data is handled. Inference runs entirely within Tamnoon’s own AWS account in US commercial Regions, so data never leaves AWS. For information about model availability by Region, refer to &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/models-regions.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Supported models by AWS Region in Amazon Bedrock&lt;/a&gt;. Because Bedrock doesn’t retain or train on prompts or completions, customer data never reaches an external vendor in the first place.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;That boundary holds even as workloads scale. Bedrock Cross-Region inference automatically routes requests to Regions within the US geographic boundary, increasing the throughput available to the application. The compute behind it stays private as well. Each service runs as an AWS Lambda function attached to a virtual private cloud (VPC), in private subnets with an explicit security group, no public ingress, and tightly controlled egress.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="guarding-what-reaches-the-model"&gt;Guarding what reaches the model&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Payloads can carry sensitive detail, so Tami applies Amazon Bedrock Guardrails to every request and response. The policy is enforced at the Bedrock layer, which means it covers all three services uniformly and no individual function’s prompt logic can bypass it. That single enforcement point does several things at once.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;First, it protects sensitive data. PII and secret-like content is masked before it is ever persisted or surfaced, so nothing sensitive lingers where it doesn’t belong. It also guards against prompt injection, which matters because Tami reasons over untrusted findings that an unauthorized user could influence. Filtering at the guardrail layer stops a crafted payload from hijacking the model or exfiltrating context. Finally, denied topics and content filters keep the model’s output scoped to security remediation and nothing else. Every intervention is logged alongside AWS CloudTrail, giving enterprise customers a clear trail of governance evidence.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="least-privilege-access"&gt;Least-privilege access&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Access across the platform runs through a dedicated per-service IAM execution role, so each function can reach only what it needs and nothing more. That principle is easiest to see in how Tami touches Amazon Bedrock. Its only Bedrock permission is &lt;code&gt;bedrock:InvokeModel&lt;/code&gt;, scoped to specific inference-profile and foundation-model ARNs. It has no ability to manage, train, or export models.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The same discipline extends to every other dependency. Access to AWS Secrets Manager is limited to the two secrets a function actually uses, SQS access is confined to its own input queue, and Amazon CloudWatch writes are restricted by namespace. There are no broad, catch-all roles anywhere in the design. Functions attach only to the managed &lt;code&gt;AWSLambdaBasicExecutionRole&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code&gt;AWSLambdaVPCAccessExecutionRole&lt;/code&gt; policies, and nothing wider than that.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="multi-tenant-isolation-and-governance"&gt;Multi-tenant isolation and governance&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Keeping tenants separated is a foundational requirement, and it is enforced from the very first call. Service-to-service calls use OAuth2 client credentials through Cognito, scoped per application, and work is processed one SQS message at a time with its tenant context attached. As a result, a single invocation only ever touches one tenant’s data.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Much of this assurance comes from treating the entire platform as code. IAM policies, network placement, model IDs, and alarms all live in versioned AWS CloudFormation and SAM templates, so every permission and model version is reviewable in source control with no manual console changes to drift out of sync. Model versions in particular are pinned to dated identifiers, for example &lt;code&gt;claude-sonnet-4-5-20250929&lt;/code&gt;, which means a model change is always an explicit, reviewed code change rather than a silent upgrade.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;That transparency carries through to runtime. Every inference call lands in AWS CloudTrail, attributable to the invoking role, and deterministic decoding with temperature set to zero keeps scoring and planning consistent from one run to the next. Failure handling is governed with the same rigor. Each service has an input queue with redrive, an error-handler function, a dead-letter queue, and CloudWatch alarms on timeouts and DLQ depth wired to on-call. Failures are contained and observable, and never silently dropped.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h2 id="results-and-impact"&gt;Results and impact&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Tami has analyzed more than 14 million cloud alerts across Tamnoon’s customer base, a number tracked in Tamnoon’s &lt;a href="https://tamnoon.io/state-of-cloud-remediation/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;2026 State of Cloud Remediation report&lt;/a&gt;. The decisions Tami makes reflect patterns learned from these real-world environments.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;For a typical customer, the results show up fast, usually within 90 days.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;table border="1px" width="100%" cellpadding="10px"&gt;
 &lt;tbody&gt;
  &lt;tr&gt;
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Metric&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt;
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Before&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt;
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;After&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt;
  &lt;/tr&gt;
  &lt;tr&gt;

  &lt;/tr&gt;
